Sites/Mission Bay

Mission Bay

OCII redevelopment area

86 of 103 tracked complete · 0 under constructionRecord as of Oct 6, 2026

Pier & Point is an independent, cited record of waterfront development. How we work →

Mission Bay was formerly underutilized Southern Pacific railyards and surrounding properties.1 The Mission Bay North and South Redevelopment Plans, approved in 1998 and administered by OCII, are converting the area into a new neighborhood; Mission Bay South alone is a 238-acre, mixed-income, transit-oriented residential, commercial and institutional district.1 Mission Bay School, the neighborhood's first public school campus, held its grand opening on Aug 6, 2026 and was scheduled to welcome its first students on Aug 17; Block 12 West, the last remaining development parcel in Mission Bay South, is the subject of a proposed OCII memorandum of understanding that had not yet been approved as of an Aug 13, 2026 advisory committee item.2, 3

DataSF business registrations show no listing for Caffe Central at this address (only one at 460 16th St, presumably a different location of the same small chain), so, like Standard Deviant Brewing, it was a real tenant missing from the registration data. View record →

    https://www.caffecentral.com/490-illinois-street-mission-bay

  • Sep 21, 2026CoverageMission BayNearby project

    New adjacent record: the SF Railyards, a 20-acre active Caltrain rail yard and the 4th & King Street Station, owned by Prologis with Caltrain holding a perpetual operating easement, adjoining Mission Bay North. SF Planning is processing it as the "SF Railyards Planning Program" (Case No. 2023-000384CWP, per the January 2025 Planning Commission packet); Prologis has floated a concept of up to 2,500 housing units and about 8 million square feet in total (about half commercial), with an 850-foot tower (SF Standard, March 10, 2026); a Development Agreement is the expected approval path (SF Planning FAQ). The map shape is bounded by real street centerlines (Townsend, 4th, 7th, King), not a surveyed parcel; see the record's accuracy note. Pier & Point records the process and does not take a position.   • Sep 19, 2026CorrectionMission Bay

    Mission Bay South Block 22 (park): status corrected from 'planned' to 'complete'. The block had been registered as a future park based on satellite imagery (a landscaped lawn with trees, no dirt, no structure), which fits either a finished small park or an interim-landscaped future site; that ambiguity should have been flagged as a question rather than resolved by assumption. An on-site visit confirmed the park is complete. A follow-up DataSF Assessor parcel and building-footprint check found no structure on the site and traced the main underlying parcel to a retired 1998 'COMMON_AREAS' placeholder plus edge fragments of the scattered UCSF campus parcel. That is consistent with open land, but it was the on-site visit, not this lookup, that settled 'complete' over 'planned'. (The retired parcel is DataSF Parcels blklot 8799TBA3, mapped 1998-07-01 and dropped 2005-09-19; read 2026-09-29.) View record →

    Hearst Tower: UC Regents' Grounds and Buildings and Finance committees approve, and vote to present to the Board, a $112,816,000 budget and external financing for the Mission Bay Block 20 Housing and Retail Project -- one 15-story tower (this building) and three mid-rise buildings (North, West and South/Student Housing), around a shared courtyard, 298,800 residential asf and 430 apartment units total -- with construction to begin June 2003 and full occupancy anticipated by October 2005

    The Regents of the University of California, 2002-11

    The Regents of the University of California, 2002-11

    The Regents of the University of California, 2002-03-14

  • May 2000StatusMission Bay

    Arthur and Toni Rembe Rock Hall: UC Regents Committee on Grounds and Buildings certifies a CEQA EIR addendum and approves the design of the Mission Bay Developmental Biology and Genetics Building (Building 19B) -- believed to be this building based on matching architect (Cesar Pelli and Associates with Flad Associates as executive architect), size (~171,000 gsf) and programs (human genetics, developmental neuroscience, developmental biology), though the Regents item does not use the 'Rock Hall' name; an $88,000,000 total project budget was approved in March 2000, with construction slated for 2001-2003

    The Regents of the University of California, 2000-05
  • May 2000StatusMission Bay

    William J. Rutter Center: UC Regents item for the Committee on Grounds and Buildings (May 2000) recommends certifying an EIR addendum and approving the design of the Mission Bay Campus Community Center Building (21B) -- this building, confirmed by matching architects (MBT Architecture as executive architect with Legorreta Arquitectos as design subconsultant) and program (fitness/recreation center, conference center, food service, retail); the item recounts that the Regents approved a $58,000,000 total project cost in March 2000 and states construction 'will begin in 2001 and be completed in 2002'. The item is the recommendation; the committee's action itself is not shown

    The Regents of the University of California, 2000-05
  • Apr 18, 2000StatusMission Bay

    Rich Sorro Commons: SF Dept. of Building Inspection permit #200004187592 filed to erect a 5-story, 100-unit residential building at 150 Berry St

    DataSF / SF Department of Building Inspection
  • Mar 1999StatusMission Bay

    Genentech Hall: UC Regents approve an amended $222.4 million capital-improvement budget, funding plan, environmental documentation and design for the 'UC Hall Seismic Replacement, Mission Bay' project (Building 24) -- believed to be this building based on matching gross square footage (433,828 gsf) and programs (structural/chemical biology, molecular/cell/developmental biology), per a January 2000 Regents item recounting the March 1999 action; site preparation began October 1999 and steel-frame construction began December 1999, per the same January 2000 item

    The Regents of the University of California, 2000-01
  • Nov 1998MilestoneMission Bay

    Mission Bay School: The Board of Supervisors adopts the Mission Bay Redevelopment Plan; a neighborhood elementary school was part of its original vision

    The San Francisco Standard, 2026-08-06; Hoodline, 2025-10
  • ~1979StatusMission Bay

    Channel Pump Station: Channel Pump Station constructed in 1979 and upgraded in 2010

    SF Planning, Sea Level Rise Vulnerability and Consequences Assessment, ch. 7 Wastewater (2020), section 7.1.1.1 Channel Pump Station, https://sfplanning.s3.amazonaws.com/default/files/plans-and-programs/planning-for-the-city/sea-level-rise/SLRVCA_Report_07.pdf
